, the faster the neuron reaches perfect alignment (see Figure
8
).

\bullet

Discussion of (ii): Lottery Ticket Mechanism for Representation Competition.

The second part isolates magnitude dynamics by assuming pre-aligned phases.
Irreps compete through their magnitudes, and the winner is determined at initialization given by
\check{\rho}_{m}=\text{argmax}_{\rho}\alpha_{m}[\rho](0)
.
Training exponentially amplifies this advantage.
Thus, the random initialization acts as a
lottery ticket mechanism
that dictates the learned representation.
The convergence speed depends on the initial spectral gap: a larger margin accelerates selection.

Under a uniform initialization, the magnitudes are
exchangeable
across all non-trivial irreps, making each equally likely to win the representation lottery.
This provides the microscopic basis for the macroscopic diversification established in Theorem
5.1
.

Takeaway of Convergence Analysis.

The training process decouples into two key subprocesses: phase alignment and representation competition.
First, the input and output phases lock into a specific rotational relationship, creating the necessary alignment for the neuron to function.
This is followed by a
lottery ticket mechanism
where the irrep with the largest initial Fourier magnitude wins and is amplified, while all other competitors decay.

6
Conclusion

This paper provides a rigorous theory of how neural networks learn group composition for finite groups, using harmonic analysis on finite groups as the analytical framework.
For general groups, including non-Abelian groups, we prove that gradient flow drives each neuron to learn a single irreducible representation with rank-one cross-layer alignment (see Theorem
4.3
), a spectral structure identified here for the first time, established via a Riemannian flow analysis on the spectral manifold.
For Abelian groups, we give a complete characterization: the learned representations and phases are independently and uniformly distributed (see Theorem
5.1
), the resulting ensemble achieves perfect accuracy through exact noise cancellation, and both phase alignment and representation competition converge exponentially (see Theorem
5.3
).

Several open problems remain, such as the theoretical characterization of limiting distributions for general groups with high-dimensional irreps or Abelian groups with self-conjugate representations.
Additionally, while we focus on the population case, a rigorous analysis of the train-test split remains open, particularly about the delayed generalization phenomenon known as grokking.

Appendix A
Flow Approximation under Small-Logit Regime

This appendix proves Proposition
4.2
, which justifies replacing the cross-entropy risk
{\mathscr{R}}
by its first-order approximation
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
in Stage I.
When the scaling factor
a
is small, the network logits are close to zero and the softmax distribution is nearly uniform, so the two risks are nearly identical.

A.1
Preparation: Gradient Computation

In this section, we compute the gradients of the cross-entropy risk
{\mathscr{R}}
in (
2.2
) and the approximate risk
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
in (
4.1
) with respect to each parameter.
These gradient expressions will be used throughout the proof of Proposition
4.2
to control the discrepancy gradient flow dynamics with respect to these two risk functions.
Recall the neural network architecture from (
2.1
):

f_{\sf NN}(g_{1},g_{2};\Theta)=\frac{a}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\xi_{m}\cdot\sigma\big(\langle\theta_{m}^{1},e_{g_{1}}\rangle+\langle\theta_{m}^{2},e_{g_{2}}\rangle\big).

We decompose both risk functions into two parts:
{\mathscr{R}}={\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}+{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}
and
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}={\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}+{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}
, where

\displaystyle{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}(\Theta)={\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)=-\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}f_{\sf NN}(g_{1},g_{2};\Theta)_{g_{1}\star g_{2}},

\displaystyle{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}(\Theta)=\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\log\bigg(\sum_{j\in G}\exp\big(f_{\sf NN}(g_{1},g_{2};\Theta)_{j}\big)\bigg),\quad{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)=\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\sum_{j\in G}f_{\sf NN}(g_{1},g_{2};\Theta)_{j}.

The first component
{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}
, which measures the negative logit at the correct label, is identical for both risks.
The two risks differ only in the second component:
{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}
is the log-sum-exp, while
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}
is the mean logit.
We compute the gradients of each component separately.

Gradients of
{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}
.

By direct differentiation with respect to each parameter, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}(\Theta)=\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}},

(A.1)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}(\Theta)=\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{1}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a}{M}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot e_{g_{1}\star g_{2}}.

(A.2)

Gradients of
{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}
.

Denote the softmax distribution induced by the logits by

({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}:=({\mathrm{smax}}\circ f_{\sf NN}(g_{1},g_{2};\Theta))_{j}
for each input pair
(g_{1},g_{2})\in G\times G
.
By direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\sum_{j\in G}({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}\cdot\xi_{m,j}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{M}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\sum_{j\in G}({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot e_{j}.

(A.4)

Gradients of
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}
.

For the approximate risk, the softmax distribution
{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}
is replaced by the uniform distribution
{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|
, which significantly simplifies the gradient expressions:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot\langle\xi_{m},{\bm{1}}_{|G|}\rangle\cdot\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}},

(A.5)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{M|G|}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot{\bm{1}}_{|G|}.

(A.6)

Comparing (
A.3
)–(
A.4
) with (
A.5
)–(
A.6
), the only difference is that the softmax weights
({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}
in the exact gradients are replaced by the uniform weights
1/|G|
in the approximate gradients.

A.2
Proof of Proposition
4.2

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

We prove this proposition by tracking the trajectory discrepancy
\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}(t)-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}(t)\|_{2}
and
\|\xi_{m}(t)-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}(t)\|_{2}
between the two flows.
Specifically, we write the ODE governing each squared discrepancy and decompose it into three terms (
A.7
): (i) the difference in projection operators, (ii) the gap between
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}
and
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
evaluated at the same point, and (iii) the Lipschitz drift of
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
between trajectories.
Each term is bounded using the explicit gradient expressions from §
A.1
.
Crucially, term (ii) is controlled by the fact that the scaling factor
a
is small: since the output is
\Theta(a)
, the softmax distribution stays close to uniform, making the gradients of
{\mathscr{R}}
and
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
nearly identical.
The resulting coupled differential inequality is solved via Grönwall’s lemma.

Error Decomposition.

Let
\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\nu}=I-\nu\nu^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
denote the orthogonal projection operator.
Let
\Theta(t)
and
\Theta^{\sf ap}(t)
denote the unique solutions of the gradient flow (
2.3
) with respect to
{\mathscr{R}}
in (
2.2
) and
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
in (
4.1
), respectively, starting from identical initialization.
For any parameter
\iota\in\{\theta_{m}^{1},\theta_{m}^{2},\xi_{m}\}
, we decompose the right-hand side of the ODE governing
\|\iota_{m}-\iota_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\iota_{m}-\iota_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}/2

\displaystyle=\langle\iota_{m}-\iota_{m}^{\sf ap},\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\iota_{m}}\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)-\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\iota_{m}^{\sf ap}}\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\langle\iota_{m}-\iota_{m}^{\sf ap},(\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\iota_{m}}-\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\iota_{m}^{\sf ap}})\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)\rangle}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(i)}}+\underbrace{\big\langle\iota_{m}-\iota_{m}^{\sf ap},\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\iota_{m}^{\sf ap}}\big(\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\big)\big\rangle}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(ii)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\big\langle\iota_{m}-\iota_{m}^{\sf ap},\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\iota_{m}^{\sf ap}}\big(\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\iota_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\big)\big\rangle}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(iii)}}.

(A.7)

In the following, we bound the growth of
\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}
and
\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}
by substituting the gradient expressions from (
A.1
) to (
A.6
) in §
A.1
into the decomposed components provided in (
A.7
).

Part 1. Bounding
\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}
.

We bound each of the three terms in (
A.7
) with
\iota=\theta_{m}^{\tau}
.

\bullet
Growth of (i) in (
A.7
).

By definition, it holds that

(i)

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}-\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}}\|_{\rm op}\cdot\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)\|_{2}=2\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)\|_{2},

(A.8)

where the second equality results from

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\theta_{m}}-\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\theta_{m}^{\sf ap}}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle=\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}^{\top}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}{\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\|(\theta_{m}^{\tau}+\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap})(\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap})^{\top}+(\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap})(\theta_{m}^{\tau}+\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap})^{\top}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}+\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\leq 2\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}.

Moreover, we can bound the gradient norm
\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}(\Theta)\|_{2}
as follows:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in G}\xi_{m,j}^{2}\cdot\sum_{x\in G}(\theta_{m,x}^{1}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(2\sum_{j\in G}\xi_{m,j}^{2}\cdot\sum_{x\in G}\big\{(\theta_{m,x}^{1})^{2}+(\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2})^{2}\big\}\bigg)^{1/2}=\frac{4a|G|^{1/2}}{M},

(A.9)

where the first inequality results from Lemma
D.1
and the last inequality uses the AM-GM inequality.
Following a similar argument, we can show that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\in G}({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}\cdot\xi_{m,j}\Big)^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{(g_{1},g_{2})\in G^{2}}\|\xi_{m}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}\|_{2}\cdot\bigg(2|G|\cdot\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}\|_{2}^{2}\}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4a|G|}{M}\cdot\Big\{|G|^{-1/2}+\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{2}\Big\}.

(A.10)

where the last inequality uses the unit sphere and the triangle inequality.
Recall that during Stage I, we fix a sufficiently small constant
a>0
as the scaling factor such that the softmax distribution
{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}
is close to uniform distribution
{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|
.
Formally, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\Delta f_{\max}:

\displaystyle=\big\|\max_{j\in G}f_{j}-\min_{j\in G}f_{j}\big\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max_{j\in G}\|f_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 2a\cdot\|\xi_{m}\|_{\infty}\cdot 2\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}\|_{2}^{2}\}\leq 8a.

By applying Lemma
D.2
and assuming that
a=o(1)
, we can bound the distribution difference as

\displaystyle\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{2}\leq|G|^{1/2}\cdot\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{\infty}\leq 16a\cdot|G|^{-1/2}.

(A.11)

Combining (
A.8
), (
A.9
), (
A.10
) and (
A.11
) gives that

(i)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8a|G|}{M}\cdot\Big(2|G|^{-1/2}+\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{2}\Big)\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16a\cdot(1+8a)\cdot|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}.

(A.12)

\bullet
Growth of (ii) in (
A.7
).

For the second term, we have

(ii)

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}^{\perp}_{\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}}\|_{\rm op}\cdot\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\|_{2},

(A.13)

where the last inequality uses
\|\mathbf{P}_{\nu}^{\perp}x\|_{2}\leq\|x\|_{2}
for all
x
if
\|\nu\|_{2}=1
.
By definition, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2a}{M}\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\sum_{j\in G}\big(({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}-1/|G|\big)\cdot\xi_{m,j}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot\Big\{\sum_{j\in G}\big(({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}-{1}/{|G|}\big)\cdot\xi_{m,j}\Big\}^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\Bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{4a|G|}{M}\cdot\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{2},

(A.14)

where the first inequality applies Lemma
D.1
and the second follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
According to (
A.13
), (
A.14
) and (
A.11
), we can deduce that

(ii)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{64a^{2}\cdot|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2},

(A.15)

\bullet
Growth of (iii) in (
A.7
).

Following a similar argument as in (
A.13
), we have

(iii)

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}.

Next, we establish the Lipschitz continuity of the gradient. For the first component, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a}{M}\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}-\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2}-\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2a}{M}\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}-\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}^{\sf ap})\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\underbrace{\Bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}^{2}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}-\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2}-\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(iii.1)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\underbrace{\Bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}-\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}^{\sf ap})^{2}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(iii.2)}},

(A.16)

where the first inequality follows from the triangle inequality and the second from Lemma
D.1
.
We now bound each sub-term. For the first,

(iii.1)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\sum_{j\in G}\xi_{m,j}^{2}\cdot\sum_{x\in G}(\theta_{m,x}^{1}-\theta_{m,x}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2}-\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(2\sum_{j\in G}\xi_{m,j}^{2}\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2},

(A.17)

For the second sub-term,

(iii.2)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j\in G}(\xi_{m,j}-\xi_{m,j}^{\sf ap})^{2}\cdot 2\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq 2\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}.

(A.18)

Based on (
A.16
), (
A.17
) and (
A.18
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{4a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}+\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\bigg).

(A.19)

Similarly, by again applying Lemma
D.1
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot|\langle\xi_{m},{\bm{1}}_{|G|}\rangle|\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}-\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2}-\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot|\langle\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap},{\bm{1}}_{|G|}\rangle|\cdot\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{\sf ap})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot\|\xi_{m}\|_{1}\cdot|G|^{1/2}\cdot\Bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}-\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2}-\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{1}\cdot|G|^{1/2}\cdot\Bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{\sf ap})^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot|G|\cdot\sqrt{2}|G|^{1/2}\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}+\frac{2a}{M|G|}\cdot|G|^{1/2}\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot 2|G|

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{4a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}+\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\bigg),

(A.20)

Combining (
A.19
) and (
A.20
) gives that

\displaystyle\textbf{(iii)}\leq\frac{8a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\bigg(\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}+\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\bigg),

(A.21)

\bullet
Combining (i)–(iii).

By plugging (
A.12
), (
A.15
) and (
A.21
) back into (
A.7
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}/2

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{16a(1+8a\big)|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{64a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{8a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\bigg(\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}+\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\bigg).

By taking the sum of the two terms, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{32a(1+8a\big)|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{256a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{16|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\bigg(\bigg\{\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}+\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\|\theta_{m}^{\tau}-\theta_{m}^{\tau,\sf ap}\|_{2},

(A.22)

Part 2. Bounding
\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}
.

We bound each of the three terms in (
A.7
) with
\iota=\xi_{m}
.

\bullet
Growth of (i) in (
A.7
).

Following a similar argument as in (
A.8
), we have

(i)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)\|_{2},

(A.23)

Moreover, we can bound the gradient norm as

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(1)}(\Theta)\|_{2}+\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a}{M}\cdot\underbrace{\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot e_{g_{1}\star g_{2}}\bigg\|_{2}}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(i.1)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{a}{M}\cdot\underbrace{\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\sum_{j\in G}({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot e_{j}\bigg\|_{2}}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(i.2)}}.

(A.24)

We bound each of these two sub-terms separately. For the first term,

\displaystyle\textbf{(i.1)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\sum_{(g_{1},g_{2}):g_{1}\star g_{2}=j}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{2}\leq\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(2\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}\bigg)^{2}=16|G|,

(A.25)

For the second term,

\displaystyle\textbf{(i.2)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}|({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}-1/|G||\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{2}+2\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2|G|\cdot\bigg(\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{\infty}^{2}+|G|^{-2}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2|G|\cdot\{1+(16a)^{2}\}\cdot|G|^{-2}\cdot 16|G|^{2}=32|G|\cdot(1+256a^{2}),

(A.26)

where the last inequality results from Lemma
D.2
based on the derived upper bound in (
A.11
).
By combining (
A.23
), (
A.24
), (
A.25
) and (
A.26
), we can conclude that

(i)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a}{M}\cdot 4|G|^{1/2}\cdot(1+\sqrt{2+512a^{2}})\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}.

(A.27)

\bullet
Growth of (ii) in (
A.7
).

Similarly, for the second term we have

(ii)

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{M}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\underbrace{\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\sum_{j\in G}\big(({p}_{g_{1}g_{2}})_{j}-1/|G|\big)\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\cdot e_{j}\bigg\|_{2}}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(ii.1)}},

Expanding the squared norm and using Lemma
D.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\textbf{(ii.1)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\|{p}_{g_{1}g_{2}}-{\bm{1}}_{|G|}/|G|\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{2}\leq 4096a^{2}\cdot|G|.

Combining the bounds above yields

\displaystyle\textbf{(ii)}\leq\frac{a}{M}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot 64a|G|^{1/2}=\frac{64a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}.

(A.28)

\bullet
Growth of (iii) in (
A.7
).

Akin to (
A.13
), we have

(iii)

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}.

(A.29)

For the gradient component
\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}
from (
A.2
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}=\frac{2a}{M}\cdot\underbrace{\bigg\|\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\{(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}-(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\big\}\cdot e_{g_{1}\star g_{2}}\bigg\|_{2}}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(iii.1)}},

where we can bound
(iii.1)
by

\displaystyle\textbf{(iii.1)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\sum_{x\in G}\big\{(\theta_{m,x}^{1}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2})^{2}-(\theta_{m,x}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\big\}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\sum_{x\in G}(\theta_{m,x}^{1}-\theta_{m,x}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2}-\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2,\sf ap})\cdot(\theta_{m,x}^{1}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2}+\theta_{m,x}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2,\sf ap})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in G}\bigg(\sum_{x\in G}(\theta_{m,x}^{1}-\theta_{m,x}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2}-\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{x\in G}(\theta_{m,x}^{1}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2}+\theta_{m,x}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,x^{-1}\star j}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 32|G|\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\},

where the last two inequalities follow from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}\leq 4\sqrt{2}|G|^{1/2}\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}.

(A.30)

Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)-\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta^{\sf ap})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{a}{M|G|^{1/2}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}-\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2}-\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2}+\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1,\sf ap}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2,\sf ap})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{4\sqrt{2}a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}.

(A.31)

Combining (
A.29
), (
A.30
) and (
A.31
) gives that

\displaystyle\textbf{(iii)}\leq\frac{8\sqrt{2}a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}.

(A.32)

\bullet
Combining (i)–(iii).

By substituting (
A.27
), (
A.28
) and (
A.32
) into (
A.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}/2

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{4a|G|^{1/2}\cdot(1+\sqrt{2+512a^{2}})}{M}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{128a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\big\{\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}\big\}^{1/2}\cdot\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}.

(A.33)

Part 3. Combining bounds and solving the differential inequality.

For notational simplicity, we introduce the shorthand

\Delta\theta_{m}=\|\theta_{m}^{1}-\theta_{m}^{1,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\theta_{m}^{2}-\theta_{m}^{2,\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\Delta\xi_{m}=\|\xi_{m}-\xi_{m}^{\sf ap}\|_{2}^{2},

Combining (
A.22
) and (
A.33
) yields the following coupled differential inequalities:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Delta\theta_{m}/2

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16a(1+8a\big)|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\Delta\theta_{m}+\frac{8a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\big(\Delta\theta_{m}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}+\Delta\xi_{m}^{1/2}\big)\cdot\Delta\theta_{m}^{1/2}+\frac{128a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Delta\xi_{m}/2

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4a|G|^{1/2}\cdot(1+\sqrt{2+512a^{2}})}{M}\cdot\Delta\xi_{m}+\frac{8\sqrt{2}a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\Delta\theta_{m}^{1/2}\cdot\Delta\xi_{m}^{1/2}+\frac{128a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}.

To decouple the system, we sum the two inequalities and apply AM-GM inequality
\Delta\theta_{m}^{1/2}\cdot\Delta\xi_{m}^{1/2}\leq(\Delta\theta_{m}+\Delta\xi_{m})/2
to absorb the cross terms.
Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\Delta\theta_{m}+\Delta\xi_{m})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot(\Delta\theta_{m}+\Delta\xi_{m})+\frac{a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot\Delta\theta_{m}^{1/2}\cdot\Delta\xi_{m}^{1/2}+\frac{a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{a|G|^{1/2}}{M}\cdot(\Delta\theta_{m}+\Delta\xi_{m})+\frac{a^{2}|G|^{1/2}}{M}.

Here, the notation
\lesssim
absorbs universal constants independent of
a
,
|G|
, and
M
.
Applying Grönwall’s inequality in Lemma
D.3
and using the fact that both flows share the same initialization, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\theta_{m}(t)+\Delta\xi_{m}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\{\Delta\theta_{m}(0)+\Delta\xi_{m}(0)\}\cdot\exp\big(\Theta(a|G|^{1/2}M^{-1})\cdot t\big)+a\cdot\big\{\exp\big(\Theta(a|G|^{1/2}M^{-1})\cdot t\big)-1\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq a\cdot\big\{\exp\big(\Theta(a|G|^{1/2}M^{-1})\cdot t\big)-1\big\},

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Theoretical Results for General Group

This appendix contains the proofs of the main results for general group learning in §
4
.
We organize the material into three parts.
First, §
B.1
derives the equivalent dynamics of the Fourier coefficients under the projected gradient flow (see Proposition
B.1
).
Second, §
B.2
proves that the gradient flow drives each neuron to a single irreducible representation with rank-1 alignment (Theorem
4.3
).
Third, §
B.3
establishes the logarithmic growth of the scaling factor in Stage II (Theorem
4.5
).

B.1
Equivalent Dynamics on the Spectral Manifold

We first employ the group DFT to translate the projected gradient flow into an equivalent dynamical system on the Fourier coefficients.
This spectral reformulation serves as the analytical foundation for the subsequent proof of Theorem
4.3
.

Proposition B.1

(Spectral Dynamics)
.

Under the approximate risk
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
in (
4.1
), the projected gradient flow (
2.3
) on the unit sphere induces the following dynamics on the matrix-valued Fourier coefficients
\widehat{\nu}[\rho]\in\mathbb{C}^{d_{\rho}\times d_{\rho}}
for each
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)
and neuron
m
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho],

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho],

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho],

where the energy functional
\Omega_{m}\in\mathbb{R}
is defined in (
4.3
).

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.

Recall from §
A.1
that the gradient of the approximate risk decomposes as

\nabla_{\bullet}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)=\nabla_{\bullet}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)+\nabla_{\bullet}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta),\qquad\forall\bullet\in\{\theta_{m}^{1},\theta_{m}^{2},\xi_{m}\}.

where the expressions are given in (
A.1
)–(
A.6
).
In this proof, we substitute the DFT expansions into these gradient expressions and simplify the results using Schur orthogonality.
Specifically, we obtain closed-form expressions for
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}
and
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}
individually in terms of Fourier coefficients (Parts 1 and 2).
We then combine the two parts, substituting the gradients into the projected GF ODE (
2.3
) to obtain the dynamics.
Finally, converting these element-wise ODEs to Fourier coefficient dynamics using the chain rule yields the claimed spectral equations (Part 3).

Part 1: Fourier Expansion of
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}
.

Recall from (
A.1
) that the first gradient block of
\theta_{m}^{\tau}
is

\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)=-\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\xi_{m,g_{1}\star g_{2}}\cdot(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}.

We now substitute the inverse DFT for each factor. Recall that, for any
\nu
, the inverse DFT gives
\nu(g)=\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\nu}[\rho]\,\rho(g)\big)
.
Inserting these into the gradient expression yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\bigg(\sum_{\rho_{1}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{1}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{1})\rho_{1}(g_{2})\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\rho_{2}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{2}}\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{2})\big)\big\}\bigg)\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{1}}d_{\rho_{2}}\cdot S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{1\tau},

(B.1)

where the last step exchanges the order of summation, and we define the vector
S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{1\tau}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G|}
by

S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{1\tau}:=\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{1})\rho_{1}(g_{2})\big)\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{2})\big)\big\}\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}.

We now simplify each entry
(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{1\tau})_{j}
for
j\in G
.
For
\tau=1
, the basis vector
e_{g_{1}}
selects the
j
-th entry via
g_{1}=j
, so the sum over
g_{1}
collapses and
g_{2}
is relabeled as
s
:

\displaystyle(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{11})_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)\big\}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)\cdot\underbrace{\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\rho_{1}(s)\big)}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small{(i)}}}+\underbrace{\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small{(ii)}}}.

For term
(i)
, note that
\sum_{s\in G}\rho(s)=|G|\cdot\ind(\rho=\rho_{\sf triv})\cdot I_{d_{\rho}}
. Then, by the linearity of trace, we have

\textbf{{(i)}}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigg(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\cdot\sum_{s\in G}\rho_{1}(s)\bigg)=|G|\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{\sf triv}).

For term
(ii)
, note that
\rho_{2}(s)=(\rho_{2}^{\vee})^{\vee}(s)
.
Applying Lemma
D.5
with
\sigma=\rho_{2}^{\vee}
gives

\textbf{{(ii)}}={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}^{\vee})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]^{\top}\big).

Combining terms
(i)
and
(ii)
, we obtain

\displaystyle(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{11})_{j}

\displaystyle=|G|\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{\sf triv})

\displaystyle\qquad+{|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]^{\top}\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}^{\vee}=\rho_{2}).

(B.2)

For
\tau=2
, the basis vector
e_{g_{2}}
selects
g_{2}=j
instead. Then, by an analogous argument, we get

\displaystyle(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{12})_{j}

\displaystyle=|G|\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{\sf triv})

\displaystyle\qquad+{|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\rho_{1}(j)\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]^{\top}\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}^{\vee}=\rho_{2}).

(B.3)

We now substitute (
B.2
) into (
B.1
) for
\tau=1
.
The indicator
\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{\sf triv})
collapses the sum over
\rho_{1}
to
\rho_{1}=\rho_{\sf triv}
, while
\ind(\rho_{1}^{\vee}=\rho_{2})
collapses the sum over
\rho_{2}
to
\rho_{2}=\rho_{1}^{\vee}
. This further yields that

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho_{2}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{2}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho_{1}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{1}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}^{\vee}]^{\top}\big).

(B.4)

For the first term, note that the sum
\sum_{\rho_{2}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{2}}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)
is exactly the inverse DFT of
\theta_{m}^{1}
evaluated at
j
, which equals
(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j}
.
For the second term, the conjugacy relation
\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho^{\vee}]=\overline{\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]}
(see Lemma
D.4
) gives
\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}^{\vee}]^{\top}=(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}])^{*}
.
Substituting these into (
B.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j}-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big),

(B.5)

where we leverage the cyclic property of trace.
Similarly, substituting (
B.3
) into (
B.1
) for
\tau=2
gives

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{2}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot(\theta_{m}^{2})_{j}-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}\rho(j)\big).

Now, we derive the gradient with respect to
\xi_{m}
using a similar approach.
Note that

(\theta_{m,g_{1}}^{1}+\theta_{m,g_{2}}^{2})^{2}=\bigg(\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\rho(g_{2})\big)\big\}\bigg)^{2}.

Expanding the square as a product of two sums indexed by
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
, and exchanging the order of summation over
(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
and
(g_{1},g_{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a}{M}\sum_{\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{1}}d_{\rho_{2}}\cdot S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2},

(B.6)

where we define the vector
S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G|}
by

S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2}:=\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{2})\big)\big\}\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{2})\big)\big\}\cdot e_{g_{1}\star g_{2}}.

For each fixed
j\in G
, the constraint
g_{1}\star g_{2}=j
allows us to substitute
g_{2}=g_{1}^{-1}\star j
and relabel
g_{1}=s
.
Then we can equivalently write the
j
-th entry of
S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2}
as

\displaystyle(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2})_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in G}\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s^{-1}\star j)\big)\big\}\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s^{-1}\star j)\big)\big\}.

Expanding the product yields four cross terms.
We first apply
\rho(s^{-1}\star j)=\rho(s^{-1})\rho(j)
to rewrite each factor involving
s^{-1}\star j
.
We group the four terms as follows:

\displaystyle(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2})_{j}

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)+\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s^{-1}\star j)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s^{-1}\star j)\big)}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(iii)}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s^{-1})\rho_{2}(j)\big)+\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s^{-1})\rho_{1}(j)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)}_{\displaystyle\textbf{\small(iv)}}.

We simplify each of these sums individually.
For the first sum in term
(iii)
, Lemma
D.5
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}^{\vee})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]^{\top}\big).

(B.7)

For the second sum in term
(iii)
,
ergodicity and (
B.7
) jointly gives that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s^{-1}\star j)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s^{-1}\star j)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}^{\vee})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]^{\top}\big),

(B.8)

Combining (
B.7
) and (
B.8
), we obtain

\textbf{{(iii)}}={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho_{2}]^{\top}\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}^{\vee}).

For the first sum in term
(iv)
,
we leverage the trace manipulation below:

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta}[\rho]\rho(s^{-1})\rho(j)\big)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\rho(s^{-1})^{\top}(\rho(j)\widehat{\theta}[\rho])^{\top}\big)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\rho(j)\widehat{\theta}[\rho])^{\top}\rho^{\vee}(s)\big).

Applying Lemma
D.5
gives that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s^{-1})\rho_{2}(j)\big)=\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\rho_{2}(j)\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}])^{\top}\rho_{2}(s^{-1})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{2}(j)\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\big).

(B.9)

For the second sum in term
(iv)
,
by an analogous argument with the roles of
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
swapped, we obtain a similar result as in (
B.9
):

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s^{-1})\rho_{1}(j)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big).

(B.10)

Thus, combining (
B.9
) and (
B.10
), we obtain

\textbf{{(iv)}}={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\Big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)\Big\}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}).

Combining terms
(iii)
and
(iv)
, we obtain

\displaystyle(S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{2})_{j}

\displaystyle={|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho_{1}]^{\top}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho_{2}]\big)\cdot\ind(\rho_{2}=\rho_{1}^{\vee})

\displaystyle\qquad+{|G|}/{d_{\rho_{1}}}\cdot\Big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(j)\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(j)\big)\Big\}\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}).

(B.11)

We now substitute (
B.11
) into (
B.6
), which gives that

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(1)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{a|G|}{M}\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big).

Part 2: Fourier Expansion of
\nabla{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}
.

Using
\langle\xi_{m},{\bm{1}}_{|G|}\rangle=|G|\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]
and substituting the inverse DFT, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{2a}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\underbrace{\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\rho(g_{2})\big)\big\}\cdot e_{g_{\tau}}}_{\displaystyle\small{:=S_{\rho}^{3\tau}}}.

For
\tau=1
, the basis vector
e_{g_{1}}
selects
g_{1}=j
, and the sum over
g_{2}
can be reduced to

(S_{\rho}^{31})_{j}=|G|\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big)+|G|\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot\ind(\rho=\rho_{\sf triv}).

By an analogous argument for
\tau=2
, where
e_{g_{2}}
selects
g_{2}=j
, we have

(S_{\rho}^{32})_{j}=|G|\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot\ind(\rho=\rho_{\sf triv})+|G|\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big).

Substituting these into the gradient expression and using the inverse DFT, we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j}+\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{\sf triv}],

(B.12)

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{2}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot(\theta_{m}^{2})_{j}+\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{\sf triv}].

In the end, we compute the gradient of
\xi_{m}
for the second part according to (
A.6
):

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{M|G|}\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\left(\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\rho(g_{2})\big)\big\}\right)^{2}\cdot{\bm{1}}_{|G|}

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{M|G|}\sum_{\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho_{1}}d_{\rho_{2}}\cdot S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{4}\cdot{\bm{1}}_{|G|},

where the scalar
S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{4}\in\mathbb{R}
is defined by

S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{4}:=\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}\in G}\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{2})\big)\big\}\cdot\big\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{1})\big)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{2})\big)\big\}.

Following a similar argument in (
B.11
), we can get that

\displaystyle S_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}^{4}

\displaystyle=|G|\cdot\sum_{s\in G}\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(s)\big)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{g_{1}\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{1})\big)\cdot\sum_{g_{1}\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{2})\big)+\sum_{g_{1}\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{2}]\rho_{2}(g_{1})\big)\cdot\sum_{g_{1}\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{1}]\rho_{1}(g_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=|G|^{2}/d_{\rho_{1}}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\ind(\rho_{2}=\rho_{1}^{\vee})+2|G|^{2}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot\ind(\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}=\rho_{\sf triv}),

where the last equality uses the conjugacy relation
\widehat{\nu}[\rho^{\vee}]=\overline{\widehat{\nu}[\rho]}
(Lemma
D.4
).
This further gives

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)\big)_{j}=\frac{a|G|}{M}\sum_{\tau\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho}\cdot\big\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{\tau}}[\rho]\big\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho_{\sf triv}]\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho_{\sf triv}].

Therefore,
\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}^{(2)}(\Theta)
is a constant vector proportional to
{\bm{1}}_{|G|}
.

Part 3: Combining and Lifting to the Spectral Domain.

Let

{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}:={\mathrm{Irr}}(G)\backslash\{\rho_{\sf triv}\}
.
We now combine the Part 1 and Part 2 results.
Adding (
B.5
) and (
B.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big),

where the trivial-representation terms cancel.
By the same cancellation mechanism for
\theta_{m}^{2}
and
\xi_{m}
, only the non-trivial representations
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}
survive.
Therefore, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big),

(B.13)

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{2}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}\rho(j)\big),

(B.14)

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta)\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big).

(B.15)

Recall that we consider the projected gradient flow as follows:

\partial_{t}\theta_{m}^{\tau}=-(I_{p}-\theta_{m}\theta_{m}^{\top})\nabla_{\theta_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\theta_{m},\xi_{m}),\qquad\partial_{t}\xi_{m}=-(I_{p}-\xi_{m}\xi_{m}^{\top})\nabla_{\xi_{m}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\theta_{m},\xi_{m}).

By substituting (
B.13
), (
B.14
), and (
B.15
) into the ODEs above, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big)-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\left\langle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}(\Theta),\theta_{m}^{1}\right\rangle_{L_{2}(G)}\cdot(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\big)\cdot(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j},

where in the second equality we use Lemma
D.7
to express
\langle\nabla_{\theta_{m}^{1}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap},\theta_{m}^{1}\rangle_{L^{2}(G)}
in terms of Fourier coefficients.
Similarly, we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\theta_{m}^{2})_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}\rho(j)\big)-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\big)\cdot(\theta_{m}^{2})_{j},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\xi_{m})_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\rho(j)\big)-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\big)\cdot(\xi_{m})_{j}.

For notational convenience, we introduce the energy functional

\Omega_{m}=\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\big)\in\mathbb{R},

which is real-valued due to the conjugate symmetry of the unitary dual
{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)
.
We call
\Omega_{m}
the energy because, as we show in Lemma
B.3
, the projected gradient flow is precisely the Riemannian gradient ascent of
\Omega_{m}
.
In other words,
\Omega_{m}
is the quantity that the dynamics seek to maximize.

Lifting to Fourier-Coefficient Dynamics.

We now convert the element-wise ODEs above into dynamics on the Fourier coefficients.
By differentiating the DFT form with respect to
t
, we obtain

\partial_{t}\widehat{\nu}[\rho]=\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g\in G}\partial_{t}\nu(g)\cdot\rho(g^{-1}),\qquad\forall\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G),\;\nu\in\{\theta_{m}^{1},\theta_{m}^{2},\xi_{m}\}.

Substituting the element-wise dynamics of
\partial_{t}(\theta_{m}^{1})_{j}
into this formula, we derive that for all
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\sum_{g\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho^{\prime}])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho^{\prime}]\rho^{\prime}(g)\big)\cdot\rho(g^{-1})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\sum_{g\in G}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho^{\prime}]\rho^{\prime}(g)\big)\cdot\rho(g^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{2a}{M}\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\sum_{\rho^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}d_{\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}

(B.16)

By applying Lemma
D.6
,we c an simplify the two terms as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}=\frac{|G|}{d_{\rho^{\prime}}}\cdot\ind(\rho^{\prime}=\rho)\cdot(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho^{\prime}])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho^{\prime}],\qquad\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}=\frac{|G|}{d_{\rho^{\prime}}}\cdot\ind(\rho^{\prime}=\rho)\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho^{\prime}].

(B.17)

We now substitute (
B.17
) back into (
B.16
).
For the first sum, the indicator
\ind(\rho^{\prime}=\rho)
collapses the sum over
\rho^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}
to the single term
\rho^{\prime}=\rho
, and the prefactor
d_{\rho^{\prime}}
cancels with
|G|/d_{\rho^{\prime}}
.
Since the sum ranges over
{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}
, this term is nonzero only when
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}
, producing the indicator
\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})
.
A similar collapse applies to the second sum.
Therefore, we arrive at

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho].

Similarly, we can easily show that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho],

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]

\displaystyle=\frac{2a|G|}{M}\cdot\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv})-\frac{2a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\Omega_{m}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho],

which characterizes the dynamics within the representation space and completes the proof.
∎

B.2
Proof of Theorem
4.3
: Representation Learning in Stage I

In this appendix, we analyze the projected gradient flow under the approximate risk
{\mathscr{R}}_{\sf ap}
in (
4.1
).
For notational brevity, we drop the subscript
{\sf ap}
.
The proof of Theorem
4.3
proceeds as follows

(i)

Riemannian Lifting
(§
B.2.1
): We lift the dynamics from Euclidean space onto a constrained manifold
{\mathcal{M}}
within the Fourier space, establishing that the projected gradient flow coincides with the Riemannian gradient ascent on the energy functional
\Omega_{m}
defined in (
4.3
).

(ii)

Critical Point Classification
(§
B.2.2
):
For a critical point
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
, we write
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}:=\Omega_{m}(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger})
for its equilibrium energy.
We classify these critical points based on the sign of the equilibrium energy
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}
and their algebraic structure.

\triangleright

Cases 1 & 2: Negative Energy
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}<0
and Degenerate Zero-Energy
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
.

We first rule out the critical points that cannot be reached from a generic initialization but are not most conveniently handled by the strict-saddle argument.
Specifically, every critical point with
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}<0
, and every zero-energy critical point
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
with support only on the trivial representation, can attract a trajectory only if the initialization lies in an embedded strict submanifold. We argue these in detail in Cases 1 & 2 in §
B.2.2
.

\triangleright

Cases 3 & 4: Non-Degenerate
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
and Higher-Rank
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
.

We then classify the remaining critical points. Specifically, we show that for every critical point with
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
and nontrivial representation support or with
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
and total rank
\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)_{\neq 1}}r_{\rho}\geq 2
, we can construct a positive-eigenvalue Hessian direction. Therefore, these critical points are strict saddles (see Cases 3 & 4 in §
B.2.2
).

\triangleright

Case 5: Rank-One
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
.
Since all four cases above are removed, we finally figure out the structure of the remaining critical points. The only remaining critical points are the (total) rank-one equilibria with positive energy
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
.

(iii)

Saddle Avoidance
(§
B.2.3
):
We prove that, under any absolutely continuous random initialization, the Riemannian gradient flow avoids all strict saddle points almost surely.

(iv)

Avoid all Bad Critical Points Almost Surely
(§
B.2.4
):
By saddle avoidance argument in §
B.2.3
, the Riemannian gradient flow almost surely escapes strict saddles in Cases 3 & 4. On the other hand, the initialization will almost surely not be in the measure-zero regions that converge to Cases 1 & 2. Consequently, the dynamics must converge to the only remaining equilibria of Case 5. This completes the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

We state the key results for each step in §
B.2.1
–§
B.2.3
, then assemble the proof of Theorem
4.3
in §
B.2.4
.
The proofs of all supporting lemmas and theorems are deferred to §
B.2.9
–§
B.2.8
.

B.2.1
Step 1: Riemannian Lifting to the Spectral Manifold

This step has two roles: it first reviews the Riemannian geometry required for constrained gradient flows on manifolds, then instantiates this framework for single-neuron Fourier coefficients within a spectral Hilbert space subject to unit-sphere constraints.

Embedded Riemannian Calculus.

We begin with the general background.
Let
\mathcal{H}
be a real Hilbert space and let
{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a real-valued functional.

Definition B.2

(Fréchet derivative)
.

We say that
{\mathcal{F}}
is Fréchet differentiable at
p\in\mathcal{H}
if there exists a bounded linear functional
\mathrm{D}{\mathcal{F}}(p):\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}

such that

\mathrm{D}{\mathcal{F}}(p)[\Xi]=\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}{\mathcal{F}}(p+\varepsilon\Xi)\right|_{\varepsilon=0},\qquad\forall\Xi\in\mathcal{H}.

By the Riesz representation theorem, there exists a unique ambient gradient

\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{F}}(p)\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying

\mathrm{D}{\mathcal{F}}(p)[\Xi]=\langle\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{F}}(p),\Xi\rangle_{L_{2}(\mathcal{H})},\qquad\forall\Xi\in\mathcal{H}.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}\subset\mathcal{H}
be an embedded manifold equipped with the metric inherited from
\mathcal{H}
.
For
p\in{\mathcal{M}}
, let
T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}
be the tangent space and let
\Pi_{p}:\mathcal{H}\to T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}
be the orthogonal projection.
The Riemannian gradient of
{\mathcal{F}}
on
{\mathcal{M}}
is the unique tangent vector

\mathrm{grad}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}(p)\in T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}
satisfying

\langle\mathrm{grad}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}(p),\Xi\rangle_{L_{2}(\mathcal{H})}=\mathrm{D}{\mathcal{F}}(p)[\Xi],\qquad\forall\Xi\in T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}.

In an embedded manifold, it is obtained by projecting the ambient gradient:

\mathrm{grad}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}(p)=\Pi_{p}\big(\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{F}}(p)\big).

Thus, the Riemannian gradient is the direction of steepest ascent after enforcing the constraint
p\in{\mathcal{M}}
.
The corresponding Riemannian Hessian is the tangent linear operator

\mathrm{Hess}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}(p):T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}\to T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}
given by the covariant derivative of this gradient field:

\mathrm{Hess}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}(p)[\Xi]=\Pi_{p}\left(\mathrm{D}[\mathrm{grad}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}](p)[\Xi]\right),\qquad\forall\Xi\in T_{p}{\mathcal{M}}.

Equivalently, using
\mathrm{grad}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}=\Pi_{(\cdot)}(\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{F}})
, the Hessian has the extrinsic form

\mathrm{Hess}_{{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathcal{F}}(p)[\Xi]=\Pi_{p}\left(\mathrm{D}\big[\Pi_{(\cdot)}\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{F}}(\cdot)\big](p)[\Xi]\right).

This expression separates the ambient second-order variation from the geometric correction caused by the movement of the tangent space along the manifold.
For a comprehensive treatment of these geometric constructs, we refer to
15
.

The Spectral Hilbert Space.

We now specialize the preceding setup to the Fourier representation of the parameters.
For real-valued parameters, the Fourier coefficients at
\rho
and
\rho^{\vee}
are conjugate and carry the same information.
It is enough to track one representative from each equivalence class.
Let
{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}\subseteq{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)
be a set such that
(i)
|{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}\cap{\rm Orb}(\rho)|=1
and (ii)
\bigcup_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}{\rm Orb}(\rho)={\mathrm{Irr}}(G)
.
Then the full collection
\widehat{\nu}=(\widehat{\nu}[\rho])_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}
is uniquely determined by the reduced collection
(\widehat{\nu}[\rho])_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}
through the conjugacy relation

\widehat{\nu}[\rho^{\vee}]=\overline{\widehat{\nu}[\rho]}
.
Thus the spectral dynamics are fully captured in this reduced half-space.
With
d_{\rho}^{\sharp}=d_{\rho}\cdot|{\rm Orb}(\rho)|
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=\bigoplus_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}\mathbb{C}^{d_{\rho}\times d_{\rho}},\qquad\langle A,B\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H})}=|G|\cdot\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}d_{\rho}^{\sharp}\cdot\Re\big(\operatorname{tr}\big((A[\rho])^{*}B[\rho]\big)\big).

(B.18)

Constrained Manifold.

Recall that in the original parameter space, the projected gradient flow (
2.3
) constrains each parameter to lie on
\mathbb{S}^{|G|-1}
.
Through the group DFT, this unit-norm constraint translates into a constraint on the coefficients
\|\widehat{\nu}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H})}^{2}=|G|\cdot\|\nu\|_{L^{2}(G)}^{2}=1
using the Plancherel theorem (see Lemma
D.7
).
Hence, we define the spectral manifold as

{\mathcal{M}}=\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{H})^{3},\qquad\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{H})=\{\nu\in\mathcal{H}:\|\nu\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H})}=1\}.

An element of this manifold is represented by the triple
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}=(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}},\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}},\widehat{\xi_{m}})\in{\mathcal{M}}
.
The following lemma shows that the dynamics in Proposition
B.1
are precisely a Riemannian gradient ascent flow on
{\mathcal{M}}
with respect to the energy functional
\Omega_{m}
.

Lemma B.3

(Riemannian Gradient Ascent)
.

The dynamical system in Proposition
B.1
coincides with the Riemannian gradient flow on
\mathcal{M}
associated with the energy functional
\Omega:{\mathcal{M}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
:

\partial_{t}\widehat{\Theta}_{m}={\rm grad}_{\mathcal{M}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}),\qquad\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m})=\sum_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}_{\neq 1}}d_{\rho}^{\sharp}\cdot\Re\big(\operatorname{tr}\big(\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{*}\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\big)\big),

differing only by a multiplicative constant
2a|G|^{2}/M
.

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

Please refer to §
B.2.5
for detailed proof.
∎

In the subsequent analysis, we absorb the positive constant
2a|G|^{2}/M
into the time parametrization and study the normalized flow
\partial_{t}\widehat{\Theta}_{m}=\mathrm{grad}_{\mathcal{M}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m})
.
This rescaling changes only the speed along each trajectory, and therefore does not affect the convergence conclusions.
The next lemma specializes the extrinsic Hessian formula above to the product sphere
\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{H})^{3}
.

Lemma B.4

(Riemannian Hessian)
.

For any tangent direction
\Xi_{m}=(\Xi_{\theta_{m}^{1}},\Xi_{\theta_{m}^{2}},\Xi_{\xi_{m}})\in T_{\widehat{\Theta}_{m}}{\mathcal{M}}
, the Riemannian Hessian of
\Omega
on manifold
{\mathcal{M}}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hess}_{\mathcal{M}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m})[\Xi_{m}]=\Pi_{\widehat{\Theta}_{m}}\big(\mathrm{Hess}_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m})[\Xi_{m}]\big)-\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m})\cdot\Xi_{m},\quad\Pi_{\widehat{\Theta}_{m}}=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\{\theta_{m}^{1},\theta_{m}^{2},\xi_{m}\}}\Pi_{\widehat{\nu}}

(B.19)

where the projection
\Pi
is defined as
\Pi_{\widehat{\nu}}(U)=U-\langle U,\widehat{\nu}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H})}\widehat{\nu}
.
Moreover, the Hessian on the full space is given by
\mathrm{Hess}_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m})[\Xi_{m}]=({\rm HS}_{\theta_{m}^{1}},{\rm HS}_{\theta_{m}^{2}},{\rm HS}_{\xi_{m}})
, where each block takes the form

\displaystyle{\rm HS}_{\theta_{m}^{1}}

\displaystyle={|G|}^{-1}\cdot\bigoplus_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}\left((\Xi_{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]+(\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho])^{*}\Xi_{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\right)\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv}),

(B.20a)

\displaystyle{\rm HS}_{\theta_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle={|G|}^{-1}\cdot\bigoplus_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}\left(\Xi_{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}+\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho](\Xi_{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho])^{*}\right)\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv}),

(B.20b)

\displaystyle{\rm HS}_{\xi_{m}}

\displaystyle={|G|}^{-1}\cdot\bigoplus_{\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}}\left(\Xi_{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]+\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\Xi_{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\right)\cdot\ind(\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv}).

(B.20c)

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

Please refer to §
B.2.5
for a detailed proof.
∎

We remark that each block is a direct sum over
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}
, so the ambient Hessian has a block-diagonal structure in the representation basis: it decouples across irreducible representations, with each
\rho
contributing an independent block.

B.2.2
Step 2: Critical Point Classification

With the Riemannian gradient and Hessian in hand, we now characterize the critical points of the normalized flow on
{\mathcal{M}}
.
A critical point is a state
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
that remains invariant under the flow:

{\rm Crit}(\Omega):=\{\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}\in{\mathcal{M}}:{\rm grad}_{\mathcal{M}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger})=\mathbf{0}\}.

For any
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}\in{\rm Crit}(\Omega)
, the notation
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}:=\Omega_{m}(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger})
denotes the energy evaluated at that critical point.
Equivalently, setting the right-hand side of the spectral dynamics in Proposition
B.1
to zero gives

\displaystyle(\widehat{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger})^{*}\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=|G|\cdot\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}\cdot\widehat{\theta^{1}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger},\qquad\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}(\widehat{\theta^{1}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger})^{*}=|G|\cdot\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}\cdot\widehat{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}\widehat{\theta^{1}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=|G|\cdot\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}\cdot\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger},\qquad\forall\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}_{\neq 1}.

(B.21)

We classify a critical point
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
as a strict saddle if the Riemannian Hessian has a positive eigenvalue:

{\rm Sad}(\Omega):=\{\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}\in{\rm Crit}(\Omega):\lambda(\mathrm{Hess}_{\mathcal{M}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}))\cap\mathbb{C}_{+}\neq\emptyset\}.

The classification proceeds by the sign of
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}
and the representation support of
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
.
The first four cases below are the excluded cases.
Specifically, Cases 1–2 can attract trajectories only from a zero. Thus, the only critical points not excluded are the positive-energy, rank-one, single-representation equilibria (Case 5), which constitute the convergence points identified in Theorem
4.3
.

Case 1. Negative Equilibrium Energy
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}<0

For negative-energy equilibria, the exceptional initialization set is

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init}=\big\{

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}_{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}:\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2},\quad\forall\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}\big\}.

(B.22)

Note this is a proper embedded submanifold of
{\mathcal{M}}
, with
\dim({\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init})<\dim({\mathcal{M}})
.
Therefore, we have
\mathrm{vol}({\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init})=0
.
The following lemma gives the dynamics of norm-gap identity.

Lemma B.5
.

For all
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}
, we denote

\Delta^{1}_{m}[\rho]=\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2},\qquad\Delta^{2}_{m}[\rho]=\|\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\|\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Then, for both
\tau\in\{1,2\}
, the evolution of
\Delta^{\tau}_{m}[\rho]
is given by

\Delta^{\tau}_{m}[\rho](t)=\Delta^{\tau}_{m}[\rho](0)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{4a|G|^{2}}{M}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\Omega_{m}(s)\,{\rm d}s\Big),\qquad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}.

(B.23)

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.

Please refer to §
B.2.6
for a detailed proof.
∎

Note that the right-hand side of (
B.23
) diverges as
t\to\infty
if
\Omega_{m}(t)
remains negative for all
t\geq 0
.
Consequently, if
\Delta^{\tau}_{m}[\rho](0)\neq 0
, then
\Delta^{\tau}_{m}[\rho](t)
will grow unboundedly, which is impossible under the unit-sphere constraint.
Building upon this observation, we establish the following result.

Lemma B.6
.

Consider the equilibrium
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
such that
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}<0
.
Then the dynamical system in Proposition
B.1
converges to
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
only if
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}(0)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.6
.

Please refer to §
B.2.6
for a detailed proof.
∎

By Lemma
B.6
, convergence to a negative-energy equilibrium can occur only if
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}(0)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init}
.
Since
\mathrm{vol}({\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init})=0
and the law of
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}(0)
is absolutely continuous, we have

\mathbb{P}\big(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}(0)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init}\big)=0
.

Case 2. Zero Equilibrium
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
with only Trivial Representation.

The same exceptional set (
B.22
) in Case 1 excludes zero-energy equilibria supported only on the trivial representation.
The precise statement is as follows.

Lemma B.7
.

Consider the equilibrium
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
satisfying
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
and
\widehat{\theta_{m}^{2}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=\widehat{\theta_{m}^{1}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=0
for all
\rho\neq\rho_{\sf triv}
.
Then the dynamical system in Proposition
B.1
converges to
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}
only if
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}(0)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.7
.

Please refer to §
B.2.6
for a detailed proof.
∎

The proof of Lemma
B.7
similarly leverages the dynamics in (
B.23
).
Since
\Omega_{m}(t)\leq 0
for all
t\geq 0
, the exponential term remains strictly greater than 1.
Thus, the trivial-representation-only pattern can only emerge and be maintained if the system is initialized within
{\mathcal{M}}_{\sf init}
.

Case 3. Zero Equilibrium
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
with Non-trivial Representation Support.

For zero-energy critical points with non-trivial representation support, the equilibrium equations reduce to triple-annihilation relations.
The next lemma records the resulting block structure and proves the existence of the positive Hessian direction.

Lemma B.8
.

Consider a critical point
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}\in\mathcal{M}
with
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}=0
.
Let
r_{1,\rho}
,
r_{2,\rho}
and
r_{3,\rho}
be the respective ranks of
\widehat{\theta^{1}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}
,
\widehat{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}
and
\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}
.
Suppose that there exist
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}_{\neq 1}
and
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
r_{\ell,\rho}>0
.
Then, these ranks satisfy the pairwise constraints:

r_{i,\rho}+r_{j,\rho}\leq d_{\rho},\qquad\forall(i,j)\in\{S\subseteq\{1,2,3\}:|S|=2\}.

Moreover, for each
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}_{\neq 1}
, there exist unitary matrices
U_{m}[\rho],V_{m}[\rho],W_{m}[\rho]\in\mathbb{C}^{d_{\rho}\times d_{\rho}}

and positive diagonal matrices
\Sigma_{1,m}[\rho]
,
\Sigma_{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]
and
\Sigma_{3,m}[\rho]

of sizes
r_{1,\rho}
,
r_{2,\rho}
and
r_{3,\rho}
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta^{1}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=V_{m}[\rho]\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{1,m}[\rho]&&\\
&\mathbf{0}&\\
&&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle(W_{m}[\rho])^{*},\qquad\widehat{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=U_{m}[\rho]\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&&\\
&\Sigma_{2,m}[\rho]&\\
&&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}(V_{m}[\rho])^{*},

\displaystyle\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=

\displaystyle U_{m}[\rho]\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&&\\
&\mathbf{0}&\\
&&\Sigma_{3,m}[\rho]\end{pmatrix}(W_{m}[\rho])^{*}.

Furthermore,
there exists a tangent direction
\Xi_{m}\in T_{\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}}{\mathcal{M}}
and a positive scalar
\lambda_{m}^{+}>0
such that

\mathrm{Hess}_{\mathcal{M}}\Omega(\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger})[\Xi_{m}]=\lambda_{m}^{+}\cdot\Xi_{m}.

Proof of Lemma
B.8
.

Please refer to §
B.2.7
for a detailed proof.
∎

Thus every zero-energy critical point with non-trivial representation support belongs to
{\rm Sad}(\Omega)
.

Case 4. Positive Equilibrium Energy
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
with Higher-rank

For
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
, the equilibrium equations force a SVD-type factorization with shared rank.

Lemma B.9
.

Let
\widehat{\Theta}_{m}^{\dagger}\in{\mathcal{M}}
be a critical point with
\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}>0
.
Then for each
\rho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)^{\sharp}_{\neq 1}
, there exist a rank
r_{\rho}\leq d_{\rho}
and three partial isometries

U_{m}[\rho],V_{m}[\rho],W_{m}[\rho]\in\mathbb{C}^{d_{\rho}\times r_{\rho}}
, i.e., matrices satisfying
A^{*}A=I_{r}
,
such that the equilibrium Fourier coefficients factorize as

\widehat{\theta^{1}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=|G|\cdot\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}\cdot V_{m}[\rho](W_{m}[\rho])^{*},~\widehat{\theta^{2}_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=|G|\cdot\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}\cdot U_{m}[\rho](V_{m}[\rho])^{*},~\widehat{\xi_{m}}[\rho]^{\dagger}=|G|\cdot\Omega_{m}^{\dagger}\cdot U_{m}[\rho](W_{m}[\rho])^{*}.

Proof of Lemma
B.9
.

Please refer to §
B.2.8
for a detailed proof.
∎

Following the factorization, the next lemma shows that every positive-energy equilibrium with total rank at least two is a strict saddle.

Lemma B.10
.

Under the setting of Lemma
B.9
, let
U_{m}[\rho],V_{m}[\rho],W_{m}[\rho]
